First of all, on 3 April 2014 the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the The European Parliament has adopted a draft Regulation No 910/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council on electronic identification and public trust services in relation to e-commerce in the internal market. This regulation operates under the name eIDAS. Secondly, that Regulation repeals Directive 1993/93/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 13 December 1999 on a Community framework for electronic signatures and legislation of individual Member States as from 1 July of this year. Thirdly, unlike the Directive, this regulation applies directly and regulates the problem equally throughout the European Union, hence the obvious need to seek legal solutions that would satisfy the regulation I have invoked. I will remind the High House that the aim of the eIDAS regulation was to increase confidence in electronic activities between citizens, businesses and public authorities, which is consequently intended to contribute to the development of electronic services, e-business, e-commerce throughout the Union. Back to the committee's report, it must be said that a number of changes of an editorial, ordering or cleaning nature have been introduced during the work on the government bill, as I have just said, using the wording used by the legislators. In addition, the wording of the Article is clarified. On the first day of the committee's work on the text of the draft law, the 28 dispute was raised. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 28 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 2 (5) indicates that a request for immediate cancellation of a qualified certificate will only be possible in justified cases. I still have to mention a number of modifications to certain amending provisions, e.g. with regard to the Road Transport Act of 6 September 2001 or the Law of 22 June 2016 amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws. It means that this law should also apply to signatures already purchased. The bill is still imprecise in many places. We are talking about provisions on trust services, Art. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5. Where and what are they? Use of certificates, Art. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4. By whom? Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5, and deletion from it, Article. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. Any action by the Ministry of Digitalization in the event of the detection of unlawful action, Article. 31. It is at least strange to impose an obligation on the NBP to implement it. Why NBP? A decision may be left to designate an implementing entity in the Ministry of Digitalization without entering NBP in the Act. Although a qualified trust service provider is required to have insurance, Article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 opens the path to providing services without this insurance. The question is who will pay the users for their losses. A qualified trust service provider shall be required to store documents and data for up to 20 years from the date of their manufacture. There is no strong enough justification for such a long period. It will be linked to the costs of the supplier, which will, after all, affect the prices of the certificates themselves. Penalties are provided for a qualified trust service provider. So we have a situation where prison punishment can be imposed on a company that is a qualified supplier. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u33	Thank you very much, Member. I would like to ask Mr Krzysztof Paszyk, a club of the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u34	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present our position on the government draft law on trust services and electronic identification on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club. Today, the revised draft law on trust services and electronic identification is the implementation of Regulation No 910/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council on electronic identification and public trust services in respect of electronic transactions in the internal market and repealing Directive 1999/93/EC. So far, only the electronic signature institution, especially the secure electronic signature, and time marking, have been fully regulated in Polish law. The eIDAS Regulation extends the range of services available and requires Member States to establish supervision of them - speaking of e-seals, e-service, seal and signature maintenance and validation service, i.e. verification of their validity. The aim of the Digitalisation Ministry will be to ensure the functioning of the national trust infrastructure, which consists of: a trusted list, a national trust service certification centre, a register of trust service providers. The national certification centre operating within the NBP will issue certificates for qualified trust service providers. The regulation will also introduce new concepts. They will use terms such as a qualified electronic signature, it will replace a secure electronic signature verified by an important qualified certificate, and a qualified electronic time stamp. In addition, the Regulation will introduce an electronic identification notification mechanism for the provision of online services. A user of electronic identification issued in one Member State of the European Union will be able to use public online services in another EU country. All these changes are extremely needed in the era of e-state and e-economy development. Unifying and clarifying these issues at EU level will improve administrative work and speed up the development of the online business sector. Moreover, the transnational aspect is important, and our citizens will be able to benefit more easily and more safely from these safeguards outside Poland, in other Member States. The law under review is the first step towards the implementation of the directive, which is certainly right and necessary. Therefore, the PSL Parliamentary Club is in favour of directing the project to further work on the committee. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u35	Thank you very much. I would like to ask you, Mr Ireneus, for profit, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u36	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on trust services and electronic identification together with the report of committees, prints 713 and 754. This draft law aims to implement European Union law, i.e. Council Regulation No 910/2014 on electronic identification called eIDAS. The main objective of this regulation is to increase confidence in the electronic form of dealing with matters by citizens and businesses with public administrations. The proposed law - after its adoption - will replace the Electronic Signature Act, which, in the light of the development of modern technologies and, above all, changes in socio-economic relations, is no longer sufficient. The current regulation included safe electronic signature and time marking. The Government's draft law on trust services and electronic identification follows the eIDAS Regulation by introducing regulations on both electronic signature and other trust services such as electronic seal, electronic time stamp, registered electronic service service, website authentication. Ensuring the security of citizens communicating with state institutions via the network and increasing their confidence in a country that does not only make it difficult, but responds to citizens' expectations, should be an absolute priority. This is all the more important, the more the development of digitalisation renders existing legal solutions obsolete. The eIDAS regulation will have a huge impact on the development of entrepreneurship in the European Union, which is why making full use of the opportunities it brings will be a key issue for Polish economic operators above all. Digitally excluded, she did not discriminate against those who were not lacking in Poland today. However, its use for the electronic circulation of documents should be gradually restricted. In the context of recent events related to the PESEL database leakage, the security of personal data and the security of online services should be a very important element both for further work on the implementation of the eIDAS Regulation and for the supervision of the application of these provisions of the Minister of Digitalisation. The civil liability of the trust service provider should be proportionate to the importance of the data at its disposal and the risks that may arise from their irresponsible or unlawful use. A thorough verification of all participants in the electronic marketing of documents is needed. This is not just a technical problem. The law must effectively protect citizens from the risk of abuse without creating excessive obstacles to the functioning of the system. Given the opportunities arising from the implementation and adaptation of the eIDAS Regulation under the conditions of the Polish legal system, as well as the issues mentioned above, the Free and Solidarnia Circle will support the draft law on trust services and electronic identification submitted by the Ministry of Development. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u42	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the bill on the works concession or service contract. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development has changed the title of the project by adopting the number of single words "the Agreement." The draft law was adopted by the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on 21 July this year. The text submitted is the result of the work of the subcommittee which was held at three meetings between 5 and 19 July. During the work of the committee and the subcommittee, there was a substantive discussion which resulted in the proposed and adopted 20 changes in order in relation to the submitted government print project No 612. A significant part of these changes is aimed at adapting the draft law to the quite extensive revision of the Public Procurement Law adopted on 22 June. Other changes are clear, legislative and are the result, among others. comments submitted by the Legislative Bureau. The proposed law constitutes the implementation into Polish law of Directive 2014/23 of the European Union on the award of concessions and will replace the 2009 law on works concessions or services. The submitted project will complement the implementation of the Polish Public Procurement Package. The purpose of the concession contract law is to ensure transparent and competitive rules for the selection of a concessionaire while ensuring the maximum permissible flexibility directives. The draft law submitted regulates four main issues: the scope of the law, the procedures for concluding concession contracts, concession contracts, in particular the scope of admissible amendments, and appeals and judicial proceedings in matters of concessions. At this point, I would like to stress that there is a fundamental difference between traditional public contracts and concession contracts. Unlike typical public contracts, concessions should be understood to mean contracts in which the economic risk of the project is assumed by the contractor. In principle, the concession holder does not receive remuneration directly from the Contracting Authority but obtains the right to operate a building or to provide services covered by the concession. In principle, therefore, the concession holder must ensure that he/she has adequate income. Any payment from the Contracting Authority may only be incidental. This fundamental difference makes the structure of concession contracts more complex than typical public procurement contracts. The concession contracts will most often serve public-private partnership projects. This justifies a different approach of the EU legislator to shape the procedures for selecting a public contract contractor and the choice of a concessionaire. The procedures for the selection of a concessionaire are much more formalised than those for typical public procurement. Therefore, the project also provides for simplified procedures for the selection of concession holders. The Contracting Authority will be able to arrange the procedure for the selection of the concessionaire in such a way as it considers convenient, while maintaining the rules of conduct, i.e. fair competition, equal treatment of economic operators, proportionality and transparency. The principle will be to conclude concession contracts after publication of the concession notice: in the Public Procurement Bulletin - in the case of contracts with a value of 30 thousand. EUR up to 5225 000 EUR, in the Official Journal of the European Union, in the case of concessions with a value of 5225 000. Euros. Only exceptionally, in three well-defined cases, it will be possible to conclude a concession contract without notice: in the case of a concession contract concluded with only one contractor; in the case of the acquisition of a work of art or artistic execution; where the earlier proceedings did not lead to the selection of the offer. Expanded scope of the bill compared to the Directive. The proposed law in relation to Directive 2014/23/EU has a broader scope, as it also covers the water and sewage sector and collective transport. The extension of the scope of this law is driven by the need to introduce uniform, transparent and competitive rules for the selection of concession holders also in these two sectors. In-house concession. The draft law provides that the conclusion of in-house concessions, i.e. the conclusion of contracts between the contracting authority and the audited entity, will be excluded from the scope of application of the law. Such a model is faithful to the model adopted in the EU Directive. Leaving the model provided for in the Directive is a different option than the one adopted in the revision of the Public Procurement Law. The Public Procurement Law provides for the award of in-house contracts on a stand-alone basis. It should be stressed that the solution adopted in the revision of the Public Procurement Law is a derogation from the model provided for in the Public Procurement Directives. It was dictated by a particular need to balance the grounds for excluding in-house contracts from the scope of the Public Procurement Law and requests to withdraw from the implementation of the in-house rules in the Public Procurement Law. This is particularly important on the basis of traditional public procurement, since the contractor receives remuneration without, in principle, bearing the economic risk associated with the performance of the contract. However, the concession holder bears this risk and therefore the acquisition of the concession itself does not constitute a guarantee of profit from the activities covered by it. Consequently, the award of a concession is more similar to the indication of the entity to perform a specific task, as is the case when a subordinate entity is entrusted with certain tasks on a general basis, i.e. permission to extend the activity of a municipal company on the basis of a resolution of a local government unit, and sometimes even permission to carry out a certain business activity, such as: the performance of the tasks of the budgetary institutions in favour of parent undertakings and units subordinate to parent undertakings. Procedures for the selection of concession holders. As I have already mentioned, the procedures for concluding concession contracts are only regulated in a framework. According to the draft law, the contracting authority will be able to conduct the procedure together with the announcement in two basic ways: firstly, in response to the concession notice, all the economic operators concerned shall submit tenders together with information for the purpose of assessing compliance with the selection criteria, which is similar to an unlimited tender, secondly, in response to the concession notice, all the economic operators concerned shall submit requests to participate in the procedure together with information for assessing compliance with the selection criteria, and then invite them to submit tenders or to negotiate and tender for economic operators, which is similar to a limited tender or negotiation with a notice. The third, but only exceptional, method will be to initiate proceedings without announcement and negotiate with selected contractors. Such a procedure will only be allowed in specific cases if, for example, The concessionaire will be entitled to exclusive rights, i.e. when there are no other contractors. The grounds for exclusion of economic operators will be modelled on the rules of the revised Public Procurement Law. The mandatory grounds for exclusion from proceedings will primarily concern criminal offences. Other grounds for exclusion will be optional. The project shall also include rules on ensuring the impartiality of persons carrying out activities on the contracting side. Where the impartiality of the conductors is questionable, they will be excluded from the proceedings. A concession contract. The draft law does not provide for detailed regulation of the content of the concession contract. In addition, the proposal only provides for general rules following Directive 2014/23 of the European Union. As a rule, contracts will only be able to be concluded for a limited period of up to 5 years. If the duration of the contract is to exceed 5 years, the duration of the concession contract will not be able to exceed that period during which the concession holder may reasonably expect the recovery of the investment for the execution of construction works or the provision of services together with the return of the capital invested. This solution aims to eliminate the possibility of concluding contracts for too long a period which for years would exclude competition between undertakings concerned by the performance of concessions. The provisions of the project include, as is the revision of the Public Procurement Law, comprehensive provisions specifying the circumstances and scope of the admissible changes to contracts. For example, minor changes to contracts will be allowed, with provisions setting out in detail which changes are considered to be significant. According to Directive 2014/23 of the European Union, the draft law also provides the same as in the revised Public Procurement Law the basis for the contracting authority's termination of the contract, e.g. where there was a modification of the concession contract requiring a new concession contract procedure or the concession holder at the time of the conclusion of the concession contract was excluded and yet the contracting authority did not exclude it, e.g. He didn't know. Appeal procedures. The proposed law amends the system of legal remedies in the area of concessions in relation to the 2009 law. In place of the current administrative road, it is proposed to adopt the legal protection system provided for in the Public Procurement Law, i.e. appeal against the contracting act to the National Board of Appeal and a complaint to the district court. Such a system is more appropriate than judicial review given the substantive similarity between ordinary public contracts and concessions. It should be noted that under EU law, the concession area is subject to the same appeal rules as traditional public contracts, and therefore the compatibility of the Polish law with the appeal directives can be best ensured by extending the provisions of the Public Procurement Law to concession contracts as well. In view of the above, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the proposed bill on concession contracts for construction works or services as developed by the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u54	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Economics and Development on the Government's draft law on works concession contracts or services, print No 752. This law is primarily intended to implement into Polish law the provisions of Directive 2014/23/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 February 2014 on the award of concessions, which should increase the participation of small and medium-sized enterprises in the performance of works concessions or services. At the same time, irregular entrepreneurs who have violated their obligations under labour law and social security and environmental protection will be excluded from their participation in the implementation of concession contracts. In practice, this should increase the number of employees employed and thus the amount of social security contributions. The introduction of new instruments in the Polish economic turnover is to be welcomed. The draft law aims to reduce legal uncertainty when concluding concession contracts and facilitate the implementation of public-private projects in the areas of infrastructure and strategic services and to ensure the best possible ratio of quality to the price of concessions. Three procedures for the selection of concession holders are therefore introduced: a limited and unlimited tender and negotiations. Another instrument is the new rules for estimating the value of a works concession or service contract. The value of the concession contract is to be estimated by the contracting authority and the estimated value will consist of the total revenue of the concession holder obtained during the period of validity of the contract, without VAT. A positive impact on investment processes will be made by simpler and more transparent procurement rules through works concession contracts or services. According to them, the investor will be able to change the concession holder without having to issue a new tender. To this end, the project introduces a broad catalogue of possible changes to the works concession or service contract, including inter alia. the long-proposed law of intervention (step-in-right), i.e. the replacement of a failing concessionaire with a new concessionaire, without the need for a tendering procedure where such a procedure is foreseen at the initial stage of the implementation of the project. This is important for most projects in the form of public-private partnerships. Although the draft law lays down detailed rules and procedures for the conclusion of a concession for works or services, as well as legal protection measures and competent authorities in matters governed by the law, business environments make a number of critical comments. In their view, there are inconclusive terms in many places, which will therefore make the application of the law more difficult. Such inaccurate records can be found e.g. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Paragraph 24 2 and Article 2 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the draft law, where there is no solution, where the application is received later than indicated, and in the provisions relating to the principle of impartiality according to which the A good solution is a similar procedure as in Public Procurement Law to appeal to the National Board of Appeal and, further, possibly to the General Court. This will certainly improve the current administrative procedure. The clarification of in-house procurement rules should also be assessed positively. The Free and Solidarni Circle appreciates the draft law well, especially in the context of the impact on the increase in the participation of small and medium-sized enterprises in the performance of concession contracts. We share the view that, due to the simplification and flexibility of procedures, this law will increase employment in companies involved in projects in the construction sector and in the services sector. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u55	Thank you very much, sir. Anyone else want to come up with a question? If not, I'm closing the list. The first question is asked by Małgorzata Płępek, the Civic Platform Club. Question time - 1 minute.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u66	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary bill amending the Law on the departments of government administration and some other laws which I am presenting to the High House could in fact be called a bill on strengthening the integration of state policy in the field of maritime affairs and strengthening the competence of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping to conduct this policy. There is no denying that this area has been treated as a stepmother by almost all previous governments, with the slight exception of the PiS governments in 2005 and 2007. The Ministry of Maritime Affairs was unfortunately wound up and its achievements were largely wasted. The issues of maritime and inland waterway management were scattered among several ministries: treasure, infrastructure, economy, environmental protection and a few more. In each of these ministries, they were side areas, secondary areas, not treated with the utmost care and with the utmost care. The strategic intention of Law and Justice, which we have already announced in the electoral campaign and which we have been implementing since the rule of law, is to give a very high priority to the promotion of maritime and inland waterway management. The expression of these actions was the creation of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping, and in this parliamentary bill we want to strengthen this Ministry, give it the necessary additional competences, so far not possessed by the Ministry, and necessary for its effective functioning. Why is it so important? Let's take a look at what this area of the ministry is doing, this area. The shipbuilding industry, for example. Now we are talking so much about the need for reindustrialisation, that in order for Poland to break out of the trap of average income, Poles should not be in the global economic division of subcontractors, contractors of small, low-paid, low-skilled jobs, only should produce ready-made, finished, precisely requiring high qualifications, high technology, valuable products, and probably hardly a more valuable and complex product than a sea-going ship. It is estimated that one job in the yard is between seven and a dozen - sometimes with more complex types of ships - jobs with co-operatives and suppliers. Moreover, they are to a large extent good jobs for qualified people, for engineers, for constructors; they are also well paid jobs. We particularly care about the development of this industry. Ports, seaport turnover is also creating additional jobs in the whole back room. Recently, a study by Dr. Matczak of the Maritime Academy has been presented, which has proved that one job in the port is six jobs in its immediate surroundings, but almost 33 jobs in the transport and industry sector across the country. The ports are already responsible for 230 grand. jobs in the Polish economy. But little of this, ports are also direct billions of revenue to the state budget due to taxes and duties paid out at the moment when goods cross the EU customs territory just in Poland. So that port development is also particularly important. There is also a branch of transport such as inland navigation. It's in the name of the ministry. We have created a new department of government in the form of inland waterway transport. It is the transport department for many freight groups optimal. This mode of transport is the most ecological, most clean, often the cheapest. It is estimated that compared to circular transport there are six - seven times more effective when it comes to noise, air pollution and five times more cost-effective. Meanwhile, this field was in a state of complete collapse in Poland, you can say, it has been eliminated. Suffice it to say that the average EU transport of goods by inland waterways is about 6% freight - the EU average, i.e. together with countries which, for example for natural reasons, do not have the conditions for the development of this type of transport at all. In Poland, a lowland country with a favourable river system, this road is transported only below 0.4% goods. Our western neighbour, Germany, transports up to 240 million tons of goods per year by inland waterways. In Poland it is only 7.5 million t, or 30 times less. Is Poland 30 times smaller than Germany? Of course not. The development of inland waterway transport, the regulation of rivers necessary for this development, brings additional benefits in the form of additional renewable energy sources, in the form of flood protection, in the form of water supply security for agriculture. Finally, the fisheries sector is also very important not only as a further tens of thousands of jobs, but also as a certain ethos, lifestyle, tradition and culture of life inherent in the Polish Coast, included in the cultural landscape. Without fishing, the Polish Coast would not be what it is. We have a great responsibility to keep this type of business, but also this kind of lifestyle, passed on from generation to generation in fishing families, to preserve and develop for future generations. I have drawn just so quickly the most important aspects to show what challenges the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping faces. We can already see the good fruitage of this ministry's activities. The High Chamber adopted the Act on Activation of the Marine Industry drafted in the Ministry, which will enter into force on 1 January and will start to produce positive results. We have developed, we are implementing a strategy for developing inland waterways for 2016 and 2020 with a view to 2030. It will allow us to reach out to the huge European measures that we will invest in the development of our waterways, in the regulation of Polish rivers, in the development of inland waterway transport. But this only shows that we should move further towards strengthening the ministry's competences, that we should give the ministry the necessary powers to conduct state policies in the field of maritime and inland waterway transport in a multidimensional manner. This law, which provides for the amendment of 29 different laws, is now in line with this need. I will not discuss all 29 paragraphs after the paragraph, but I think that the proposed changes could be grouped into these four main groups. First of all, it is the granting of the Minister of Maritime Affairs authority over the state institutions in its respective area, such as the Maritime Search and Rescue Service, the Central Maritime Examination Commission and the Maritime Institute. Secondly, this is to establish the general competence of the Minister for Maritime Affairs as a minister exercising the powers of the Treasury in the State Treasury of companies in the field of maritime affairs, fisheries and inland waterway transport, as well as to include this particular minister in the laws which constitute the activities of some of these companies. There are companies that have their dedicated laws that regulate their operation, e.g. it is a port and marina law for companies managing seaports. The third of these groups is to include the consequences of the establishment of an IWT division in a whole range of legislation on transport, transport security, environmental safety, exceptional conditions, etc. Finally, the fourth is the appointment of the Minister as a body of substantive supervision, consultation in upper secondary education, in research on the areas specific to the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. High Izbo! This law is another step towards strengthening the state's policy to promote the development of the maritime economy, the development of shipyards, ports, fishing and inland waterway transport, which should be at our heart, which can become a flywheel of the Polish economy. We can see now that the Ministry has proved successful in its activities. We also need to help, give additional tools to develop these areas that are so important to our economy, which are even faster, even more efficient, with even greater success for our country. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u78	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members' Circle of Free and Solidarni, I present my position on the matter of the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on the departments of government administration and certain other laws. Establishment in December 2015 The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping, whose tasks include handling these three departments, was the first step in the right direction. Another appropriate step, which the provisions of the proposed regulation provide for, is to extend the competence of the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport to the extent necessary to enable him to properly fulfil his obligations. In addition, project promoters propose to introduce a number of regulatory changes which are logical as a consequence of the rules already laid down and the political course chosen. These include the gradual transfer of tasks in the management of the State Treasury to the competent ministries of industry, the harmonisation of laws in the area of entities under the supervision of the Minister responsible for Maritime Affairs, the inclusion of the Maritime Institute in Gdańsk under its supervision and the transfer of certain competences currently belonging to the Minister responsible for Transport to the Minister responsible for Inland Navigation. Ensuring consistency in the operation of the relevant ministries and the substantive distribution of competences is, in my opinion, crucial to achieving the target set, which in this particular case is the re-bloom of the Polish maritime and inland waterway economy. As irrational regulations in this Act, the Polish Districts Association indicated provisions giving the right to competent ministers to establish and run public schools of sea, inland waterway and fishing. [[Applause]] So that they are those supposedly incompetent persons who have been appointed to serve in port companies. The second thesis or the second major problem is the involvement of local authorities in managing ports. This, of course, is a problem and an important issue that we should lean on. The intention - this is how I read it, because, for example, because of age, I was not there when without a small 20 years ago the Law on marinas and seaports was passed - the legislator was so as to actually involve the local government in managing strategic ports. I think that here the legislator correctly saw a discrepancy to some extent, let's say, interests or natural directions of action between ports and local authorities in which they lie. Because while it is in the interests of the whole state and the economy of the whole state that the maximum development of ports is in the interest of the whole state, the development of transhipments in ports, while the cities in which these ports lie may sometimes have a slightly different idea. They may think that, for example. excluding any land from the maritime economy and selling it under a lucrative development contract will be a more advantageous option for one or another, the mayor, the president. The pressure of housing, the displacement of industrial areas, sometimes located close to the city centers, is a natural contradiction. The intention was for local authorities to engage in port management, to feel co-hosted, so that this natural conflict of interest would be eliminated. The records originally included in this Act provided, first of all, that local authorities were to engage in capital ports either through the import of funds or through the transfer of land to companies managing ports. At the same time, unfortunately, there was no conclusion that they would have a significant role in the supervisory bodies in return. Unfortunately, it's hard for the local authorities to be surprised, because they're here to do their own business, if that's what they're allowed to do... There was no dependence on one another, i.e. those supervisory powers, but they did not engage capitalally in port management companies. And here in the normal market economy, we would have to go to the entrepreneur and ask him if he would agree to something like that, that he would appoint a company to which he would bring. 99.7% the capital and the other party will raise 0,3% of the capital and will mid-control the supervision of the company and will have the President of the Supervisory Board from the vending machine. Unfortunately, such situations in port management companies are happening and it is simply strange and abnormal, so that the grossly minority shareholder has such an impact on the operation of the company. I would very much like to encourage... I do not want to speak here on behalf of the Ministry, I do not have the authority to do so, but I think that the Ministry would be happy to take talks with local governments if they wanted to contribute to the inclusion of additional shares or especially to transfer additional land for the development of the maritime economy, for the development of ports, to increase their involvement in these companies, we will certainly be happy to carry out such talks. However, in a situation where there is no such will, it cannot be that a shareholder with less than 1% shares have half control over the company. The next voice that appeared is the alleged archaic concept of the Ministry of Poland. No, I would say the opposite, that it is archaic that all companies of the Treasury are to be in one specialised ministry, which in principle is to be more or less favorable, often adversely, sell. Those days are over. Companies that have been in the hands of the State Treasury, almost all, at least those from the maritime economy that we are talking about now, are dressed in the form of a company - sometimes in the world. port managements are administrative authorities, these are not companies - organisations, institutions implementing state policy in the area. I can't imagine the situation, especially after the privatisation process of transhipment terminals, where the port management, which only has to manage the infrastructure, collect port fees, invest in quays, in incoming channels, was privatized. No, he is to be a tool in the hands of the State, in the hands of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs, which is responsible for the maritime affairs of the State, and should therefore belong to the Minister responsible for these matters. Nothing more wrong. It doesn't mean that it doesn't have competence now, that it doesn't work or work badly, but we want it to work even better, even more effectively. As I say, on the basis of some experience, areas can be found, several areas in 29 laws, where such additional competences should be given to the Ministry. I also assure you that the applicants worked on this law in communication and in contact, consulting, also using the government's help, in the preparation of the content of this project. It is clear that the scope of government departments cannot be regulated in isolation or without contact, without consulting the government that will work on this basis. What's causing this? This violates the basic principle of the tax on goods and services, i.e. the principle of neutrality. This causes smaller local governments, smaller municipalities and small towns to lose. This creates a legal gap whereby law firms will still claim from the State Treasury and deduct input tax. How can we bring about a situation where a local government unit carrying out the same activity before the entry into force of this law will be able to exercise the right of deduction and a local government unit which, in relation to [[♪ The bell ♪]] for the same activities, but on the basis of the new law will be in a worse legal situation? The problem is not only the situation of sick pigs - it is clearly described in international and national regulations - but also what to do with those animals that are on farms located in zones. So far, there has been a problem with the development of valuable pork meat from these zones, because private entrepreneurs have not been interested in buying in, and the ability of public finance operators to buy in was limited by the need for complex tendering procedures. This law, once again, as a special, extraordinary tool for the government to be able, through negotiations, to select entities - from the Ministry's information, it appears that they are already willing to do so - to order them to slaughter and process the meat of animals from flocks located in protection zones is an important and acceptable solution by Members. This, of course, is not the only solution. A new large law is announced which will regulate the rules for dealing with situations such as this unprecedented problem of African swine fever in Poland. On the other hand, today it is important to solve the problem of the development of these animals and under different conditions, detailed and described, this law introduces such solutions. Therefore, the Committee on Agriculture, after first reading and considering this draft law, as I mentioned, at its meeting today, asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u137	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the Act on Special Solutions for African swine fever in the territory of the Republic of Poland, in print Nos 793 and 799. This project is processed in a very fast, extraordinary mode, because that is the need of the moment. The epizootic situation of ASF in Poland is extremely dynamic and requires extraordinary decisions, including those related to lawmaking. The main objective of the bill is to ensure the possibility of developing products of animal origin intended for human consumption, produced from meat of virus-free pigs, i.e. healthy but present in areas where restrictions have been introduced due to the presence of this virus in Poland. Poland is required, in accordance with European Union decisions, to take specific measures in designated areas to combat and prevent the spread of the virus. These recommendations also apply to healthy animals and aim primarily at eliminating the risk of the spread of ASF virus and the emergence of its new outbreaks. It should be stressed that, in the case of this disease, even sick animals do not pose any risk to humans, the more any risk to human health and life does not create healthy animals from areas subject to restrictions, as well as meat from such animals or products made from them. Meat coming from such areas before processing and placing on the market shall be subjected to a detailed examination carried out by veterinarians and specially marked. The pigs free from ASF virus may therefore be slaughtered and the meat obtained from them may be further processed, processed and placed on the market. These conditions underpin the proposed legal changes which eliminate unnecessary barriers to the marketing of processed pig meat from healthy pigs kept in areas where cases of ASF have been identified. This elimination of barriers will consist in the non-application of the provisions of the Law of 29 January 2004. The right to procurement for the supply of meat products exclusively from pig meat originating only from pigs kept at least 2 months prior to slaughter in agricultural holdings located in areas covered by orders, prohibitions, restrictions and other control measures. The order under this procedure will only be awarded to operators who produce food in accordance with the veterinary rules in force and the meat products they order themselves must comply with the veterinary requirements laid down in the rules on products of animal origin. Operators interested in the performance of the contract will be required to purchase pigs at net prices not lower than the net purchase prices of hot mass in the relevant class of the EUROP classification scale in the region in which the pigs have been acquired or an intermediate price for all classes in live weight in the region. This will ensure that a specific balance is maintained between contracting stakeholders and pig producers and eliminate solutions that could exploit their difficult situation. In order to apply this derogation, it will be necessary for the contracting authorities to demonstrate that the subject matter of the contract is related to safety and public order, to life or to human health or that the amount of the contract is less than the amounts set out in the Public Procurement Law. These amounts are less than 135 000. EUR or 209 000. the euro, depending on the legal status of the enterprise. The Act will allow for action in this area without the characteristics of freedom and will ensure transparency of the contracts awarded. It shall also describe the requirements to be met by the applicant. The situation in Poland concerning ASF is specific and requires specific solutions to mitigate the adverse effects of this disease also in the economic sphere. These solutions will benefit both farmers and will mitigate the particularly difficult situation of food-producing operators from such meat, so they will bring tangible benefits for the economy and the labour market as a whole. The alternative in the event of excessive disposal difficulties, the destruction of meat from healthy pigs from restricted areas is, in our view, an alternative incomparably worse. With all these arguments in mind, the Law and Justice Club will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u169	Thank you very much. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Mr Jacek Bogucki, Minister of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, will now speak. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u170	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I will answer the questions that arose during this difficult debate, difficult because the atmosphere is indeed - and here I will agree with Minister Sawicki - a serious one and we must be very humble about this situation. Spain was mentioned, which fought for 35 years and managed by killing more than 2 million pigs in Catalonia, banning any production on farms and creating large, powerful industrial farms that resumed this production. In this way Spain became an exporter of pigmeat. Poland ceased to be an exporter of pigmeat at the same time. We believe that such a direction, in the situation of Polish buildings in the countryside, is difficult to accept, threatens the environment and is not a solution that is possible under Polish conditions. Therefore, while continuing what has been done in this area so far, we believe that it is necessary to protect family farms, good farms, those which already exist in Poland, protecting the environment, but above all protecting the Polish breeding of pigs. And the whole package of actions since the first day that Prime Minister Beata Szydło formed, since Krzysztof Jurgiel became Minister of Agriculture, has been moving in this direction. Firstly, we have tried to fight effectively - and here I answer the question - with an excessive population of wild boars, and in areas where there is a threat of disease development, these actions would lead to a radical reduction of this population. Unfortunately, the solution adopted in the form of voluntary declarations of hunting circles and agreements signed with county veterinarians in the field of wild boar shelling did not result in this objective being achieved. Therefore, among the solutions adopted today at the government meeting in the package of actions contained in the second specialty is the possibility for district veterinarians to impose an obligation to fire wild boars. This should eventually lead to a situation where the wild boar population will be reduced. But we must be aware of how sneaky the virus is and difficult to fight, because surrounded by the sea of Sardinia, the island in relation to the territory of Poland is small, nor can it cope with this problem and even though it would seem that all animals carrying the disease could be killed there and can be recreated from the beginning of breeding, it did not happen. Poland is an open territory. East of Poland, this disease rages in a shocking way. Recent cases in Ukraine, near the Polish border, have caused extraordinary action by the European Commission, among which is the declaration of the European Commission helping Ukraine to counter the development of this disease. We do not know the situation in Russia, Belarus, because the services there do not inform the European services in this regard. The situation is much more difficult than in Poland in Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, where stocks of dozens, dozens of thousands of pigs were slaughtered, so this number, which was in Poland until this year - in the case of boars there were about 100 cases, in the case of pigs 3 cases - is really, given the development of this disease in other countries, a small number. But this has led to the emergence of other, new secondary sources of ASF infection, because most of the cases that we are talking about at the moment have of course a primary source: the transfer from boars to pig farms, or actually to piglets, but then there was illegal sale of these piglets, disbanding them after a large part of the Podlaskie voivodeship and buying by farmers without certificates which were already mandatory during that period. So there was a spread of a road that did not exist in Poland before. There are many ways to spread this virus, and it was mentioned here: through straw, through various types of pets. It can also be carried out in some cases - in other countries - through feed. So this really is a very difficult fight because none of the ways will rule out ASF disease in pigs because the virus is within the reach of a given country or it can enter this country from outside its borders. That's what we all need to be aware of. There was no relaxation of discipline. The services have carried out their activities as they have done so far. But maybe over the past two and a half years our fight against ASF has run out of consciousness that it is such a dangerous disease and can so easily move to further herds. Maybe there was a little bit of a whispered information campaign among farmers, that's the problem. Wild boars only at the eastern border, maybe other factors worked. Nor do we exclude sabotage and such a way of spreading this disease, because the competent services are currently investigating this case and the final results are not yet known. It cannot be alleged that, when a large fire was found, an immediate decision was taken to slaughter this stock and to transport it to the nearest establishment engaged in such activities under full safety conditions. You can't accuse because for years, and not for the last 8 months, 10 months or 8 years, but for the last 27 years, the combs have been destroyed in Poland. The obligation to run them is the responsibility of the municipalities. There's no such place as this. And let me remind you that the mentioned Spain buried the animals at the place of slaughter. In Poland there is no basis for doing this. These foundations must also be restored and will be restored in the second project discussed today by the Council of Ministers. There are also no state-owned plants involved in such disposal, because the entire agricultural environment has been privatised as part of the wild privatisation process. This is also the completion of these twenty years of transformation in Poland. Establishments dealing with e.g. such activities should be owned by the State not so that the State makes money from them, but so that, in such difficult situations, there is a mechanism enabling it to execute such an order immediately by voluntary means without looking for a contractor through a tender. Indeed, comprehensive solutions are needed, and under the applicable law, all possible solutions for over one month of disease reappearing in pigs and for months since November. r., have been implemented and undertaken. The issue of animal records also needs to be amended. Here, in answer to further questions, I can say that also in this respect, the project provides for sanctions against farmers who do not mark or report their animals to the system, but on the other hand does not work backwards, and so allows the farmer to register his flocks by the date specified in the law, to arrange their status. Moreover, ahead of the law, we sent a request to all farmers with registered flocks in the area covered by any restriction to verify their data contained in this system, because it was indeed for years that the problem of lack of records and irregularities in them was growing, because in Poland it was up to 270 thousand. holdings declared in the register are 35 000. holdings which have a negative number of pigs. It's physically impossible, it's mathematically impossible, it's agriculturally impossible, but this condition has been tolerated for years and this problem has been getting worse, because every year after that, it's brought more irregularities, more differences between the facts and the records. These turbulent proceedings we have been dealing with today are only part of this storm that is already going on in the country. I've been going all month to meetings with farmers at processing plants. The meetings with the officials were really difficult, but they were substantial. Farmers pointed out these solutions, which they believe should be taken into account in order to resolve the problem, and which should have been taken long ago and without which we will not be able to deal with the problem. So this upset at today's committee meeting is also the result of the situation in which we have been actually in Poland for 3 years, because, as I said, alongside the internal threat, we also have huge threats from the eastern part of Europe, where the problem with ASF is even more complicated. As a result of the interministerial team set up by the government, all services have taken enhanced action in their area of competence. We try to fully control all markets, all possible routes of illegal transport of animals, but we must remember that we live in a democratic country in which moving around the country is not prohibited, is acceptable, and the history of Poland shows that even in difficult times when meat was banned, it was forbidden to raise animals without reporting to the occupation authorities, later the Soviet authorities, earlier the Nazi authorities, Poles dealt with this problem if they had to cope. Therefore, we must equip Polish farmers with support. In addition to the restrictions, there must be support for those who give up small farms, and at the same time support for those who should and must protect their farms because they want to continue breeding pigs, they still want to raise pigs. In this climate, we discussed these issues last Thursday with Minister Krzysztof Jurgiel, Commissioner Andriukaitis. The talks were about support as well. The European Commission for Polish activities. There is full understanding, there is full respect for Poland, that Poland can so far defend Europe and a large, significant majority of its territory against the development of this disease. After all, scientific information indicated that the spread of this disease occurs at a rate of between 100 km and several hundred kilometers per year. After all, some scientists said a year ago that wild boars weren't meant to be blown away because those sick people were going to die out, I'm sorry, they're dying out, I'm already infected with correctness to a certain extent, they're dying out, and healthy animals are going to come to this place. Scientists learn to fight this disease not only in Poland, but also in the world, and so far the effectiveness is not so great. Professionals learn, servants learn, farmers learn. Unfortunately, Poland has got to learn from its mistakes, it has to learn from its own body. Among the actions that have already been taken and which have already been included in the legislation is a whole set of what you are talking about. In the bill, which I hope will be in the Sejm next week, and today has been or will be adopted by the government, is the solution to the problems of sealing the rotation and shooting wild boars. We are discussing the creation of a program, the principles of a compartment that would allow the separation of farms that would meet very strict breeding conditions, and regardless of what zone would have been created in the area, they would still be able to carry out their activities. It is important to remember that not only in the territory of the free part of Poland, but also in the area where the disease is already rampant among wild boars, and has also appeared among pigs, there are good farms, protected farms, large farms, there are farms which must produce pigs because they have borrowed for this purpose, because they have developed their farms, and they must get the support of the state. This is the set of actions that are being implemented. We have managed to persuade the European Commission to come up with a map. The most restrictive restrictions are in the blue zone, which is relatively small. In the red zone and then in the yellow zone there is simply increased care, bioinsurance, there are stricter conditions for animal breeding, but without commercial restrictions. Because this bill is intended to reduce its effects, but also to ensure that illegal trade in pigs in the territory of the voivodships covered by the zones is simply not economically profitable. As soon as we pass this law, it will not make sense for a farmer to drive his pigs out of the area and sell them cheaper if he can obtain a high average price in the region for legal sale from the farm, regardless of the size of the farm. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u172	Dear Marshal, Dear Members, I would like to thank you very much for the opportunity to present the annual information at the meeting of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. At the beginning of my speech, I would like to thank my colleagues, my alternates and the Ombudsman's Office very warmly for their support, because what I will talk about would not have been possible if it had not been for the active and committed work of my office staff. The Ombudsman shall be the constitutional body which, in accordance with Article, shall be the constitutional body. 208 of the Constitution performs the tasks of upholding civil rights and freedoms, but these tasks are defined not only in the Constitution but also in other normative acts. In this context, particular attention should be paid to the obligations and tasks performed by the Ombudsman's Office under the Optional Protocol to the UN Convention on the Prohibition of Torture, Inhuman and Demeaning Treatment. These are tasks in the form of the National Prevention Mechanism, i.e. taking visits to check all places of imprisonment in Poland. Secondly, the Ombudsman is a monitoring body for the implementation of the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, and thirdly, the Ombudsman also has equal treatment tasks, which result not only from the Constitution, but also from the Act of 3 December 2010 on the implementation of certain provisions of the European Union on equal treatment - here the Ombudsman shares these powers with the government's proxy for equal treatment in accordance with Article. 18 of the Act, the scope of the Ombudsman's action on the grounds of constitutional and statutory competence is different from that of the Government. The role of bodies such as the Ombudsman is to draw lessons from this and act for citizens. The Ombudsman should be active, see problems, look for solutions, wonder how to help. There are still many unresolved problems, which are my concern and concern, and which are also indicated during regional meetings, meetings with NGOs, citizens. A large part of my mission is to constantly meet, consult and talk, to know what and how to react, and to react quickly, if possible but professionally. I would like to stress that the Constitutional Tribunal is a key body for the protection of the rights and freedoms of the individual, which proves in its recent case law. I would like to point out here the judgment of 28 June 2016 on the incapacity I was talking about. The Ombudsman's annual information and reading shows how important this role is and why the independence of the Court and its effectiveness are important in terms of protecting the rights and freedoms of the individual. I am grateful for the positive cooperation of other spokespersons and public administrations, as well as non-governmental organisations. Cooperation, an attitude towards the protection of individual rights and respect for dignity can really solve a lot of issues. Human rights are, contrary to appearances, a rather complicated area. This is an area that requires really great expertise and commitment to carry out various changes. This cooperation is sometimes necessary. Unfortunately, many problems cannot be solved without legislative intervention. It is also important to make active use of the standards resulting from international agreements ratified by Poland. It is these standards that often show what our national system is failing, what could be solved, how to make citizens living better in different parts of our country. This is demonstrated by the number of interventions undertaken, but also by Adam Bodnar's activity in contacts with people, which has not yet been seen by the Ombudsman, the number of trips and meetings. The number of applications to examine the constitutionality of the laws to the Constitutional Tribunal was also significant. It's a proof of that. Unfortunately, there is a great deal of activity in violation of the Constitution and civil rights. The Ombudsman's educational activity is also important. In conclusion, in 2015, a total of 57,627 applications were submitted to both ombudsman and ombudsman, including 27,376 new applications. In addition, the Ombudsman's office adopted 5656 persons as well as 38 074 persons in telephone calls. For many of these proposals, this was often the last chance for citizens to make a positive solution, to defend their rights effectively. The Ombudsman was their last resort. In 2015, the Ombudsman took 506 cases on his own initiative. The Ombudsman's 144 contributions concerned the need for a legislative initiative to remedy these various alleged infringements. Using its own capacity, the Ombudsman addressed 21 applications to the Constitutional Court, joined 12 proceedings initiated by constitutional actions, as well as five proceedings initiated by a judicial question and four proceedings initiated by other entities. Following the disclosure of discrepancies in the case law of the general courts, the Ombudsman addressed four questions to the Supreme Court in 2015, as well as other courts, e.g. the administrative court. The Ombudsman shall also carry out tasks relating to the implementation of the principles of equal treatment entrusted to him under the provisions of the Law of 3 December 2010 on the implementation of certain provisions of the European Union in the field of equal treatment. As part of these tasks, reports and analyses have been issued, such as: The availability of lessons of minority religion and ethics in school education, the availability of academic education for people with disabilities, the recognition of family and professional roles. Equal treatment of parents in the labour market. The Ombudsman appointed a substitute for equal treatment and the Equal Treatment Team was separated from the office's organisational structure. In addition, the Ombudsman's office continues the work of expert committees dealing with the implementation of the principle of equal treatment: the Committee on Elders, the Committee on Migrants, the Committee on Persons with Disabilities and the Committee on Homelessness set up in 2015. Adam Bodnar also launched a programme of regional meetings - he drives around Poland to meet with citizens and help them solve their problems. He has had more than 30 visits since November 2015. The subject matter of his intervention was very diverse and it is not possible to mention the whole spectrum of issues he dealt with. Among other things, he helped a person who was hit by a car who did not receive an accident pension, intervened on acts of chauvinism, intervened on the minimum wage, and the largest number of cases that the Ombudsman dealt with concerned the rights of disabled people and the issues of NGOs. He was also involved in protecting those who were discriminated against and rejected - seniors, homeless people, disabled people, people with non-traditional sexual orientations or Muslims. He fought for the defense of homeless people. After Adam Bodnar's position as an ombudsman in the office, an organizational unit - the Centre for Social Projects - was also established in order to cooperate with civil society organisations. As part of this cooperation, regional consultations are organised in individual voivodships - meetings of the spokesperson and office staff with organisations and social leaders. It is not my goal to summarise 740 pages of the 2015 report, but it is worth noting that Dr. Adam Bodnar is a very active advocate of civil rights, and at the same time he is an advocate of civil rights close to the citizen, and the information presented to us about the activities of the Ombudsman for 2015 and the state of respect for human rights and freedoms shows these features. I must say that so far, the Ombudsman has rarely met people so often, met people in the field and traveled all over Poland. Despite these obvious evidences of the ombudsman's full oath by Dr. Adam Bodnar, we see at the right hand the temptation to attack the ombudsman, not a fair criticism to which everyone can always be exposed, but just attacks. Modern is clearly against this and will always oppose the attack on all independent institutions of the rule of law, as will all attacks on the Constitutional Tribunal or the judiciary. Thank you, Mr Spokesperson, for this year's work and we hope for further work until the end of our term of office, and let us remind you that the Ombudsman's term of office is only three years from now. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u286	Dear Marshal, Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, My task is to provide information from the activities of the National Judicial Council in 2015. I would like to inform you that in May of this year the National Judicial Council prepared written information about its activities, which was sent to the Sejm. I would like to present this speech today in a more brief form, focusing on those issues which seem to be the most important for the activities of the National Judicial Council. In the title of introducing brief information about what the National Judicial Council is dealing with, what its cognition is and how technically it looks, because I think it will explain a lot of issues that may be questionable. The National Judicial Council is a constitutionally empowered body and has the task of upholding the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. As we remember, the Constitution also defines the composition of the National Judicial Council. It consists of 25 members, mostly elected members, judges and parliamentarians. This significantly affects the functioning of the National Judicial Council, which acts as a collegial body through the adoption of resolutions. In our current activities, we must take into account that the members of the National Judicial Council continue to carry out their duties as Members, senators and judges, so that limits us to the possibility of holding meetings of the National Judicial Council. They are organised well in advance so as to give the possibility for individual members of the board to perform these functions at the same time. However, there were a number of meetings last year, a total of 17 meetings, took 57 days, so on average it is more than one meeting per month. Nearly 1400 resolutions were adopted at these meetings. I would like to stress that all members of the Council are involved in the work that is being done in the Council. In these plenary sessions, we usually have about 20 members. In addition, all members of the Board shall participate in the work of the teams which shall be appointed by the Chairman of the Board. Statistics show that such teams were designated about 500 last year. They deal with both the preliminary assessment of the candidates, which later the National Judicial Council presents to the President for appointment as judges, as well as the preliminary opinion of the draft legislative acts which are presented to the National Judicial Council for opinion. I would also like to draw attention to the fact that the National Judicial Council has limited legal cognition and therefore does not address all issues which relate to the functioning of the judiciary. He has only those tasks and powers that come directly from the bill. Such fundamental tasks of the National Judicial Council are to present candidates for free judicial positions to the President, and this takes the most time to act in practice. In 2015 they applied to serve as a judge in various competitions almost 2 000. people, and the National Judicial Council recognized almost 5 thousand. Notifications. For the sake of clarity, this involves the fact that last year there were still some competitions which took place according to the old rules, where it was possible to submit their candidacy simultaneously in several competitions, as well as new ones where restrictions were introduced. Currently it is possible to apply for only one competition until the end of the competition, so the number of those submitted was higher than the actual number of people who took part in these competitions. In 2015, there were a little more than in 2014, as it was then 1860 people. Mr President, the National Judicial Council has appointed more than 500 judges, exactly 516, so if we take into account that there are about 10,000 judges in Poland, we can say that this is a significant number of people to be appointed every year by the President, but some of them are judges who are appointed to higher courts, and some are appointed for the first time to serve as judges. I would like to draw attention to this, because this is quite important for the functioning of the courts. We must take into account here that this nomination process takes quite a long time, and that means that about 500 judicial posts are de facto not in place during the year, because that's more or less how long this nomination process lasts. If we still consider that a large number of judges These are young people, including recently dominated by ladies, who often benefit from maternity leave later, this problem of the smooth filling of vacant judicial positions seems to be very important. The National Judicial Council attaches great importance to this. Here I would like to stress that in terms of the time when this application was processed by the National Judicial Council, this time has, fortunately, been quite meaningfully shortened over the last few years. In 2014, the average duration of one notification was just over 80 days, in 2015 - on average 65 days, i.e. there was quite significant progress here. At the same time, it can be seen that the duration of this competition for the casting of a free judicial position at the moment is actually not related to this stage of proceedings before the National Council of Courts, but to the earlier stage, i.e. the announcement of a minister of the free judicial position and the subsequent execution of a procedure which is pending before the competent court, where the candidate submits his/her person. Here, we would see as the National Judicial Council some more scope for speeding up this procedure. As I said, we attach great importance to this issue. This is not only about selecting the best candidate in the competition for a free judicial position - we also pay attention to this because the problem of the cast of judges is also a problem of a systemic nature, because it is assumed that the method of appointing judges is one of the guarantees of independence of judges and independence of the judiciary. As far as these resolutions are concerned, they have it to themselves that, as we know, they are not final at the time of their adoption. Any person who is a candidate for an open judicial position shall have the right to appeal to the Supreme Court in the event that the resolution disputes. As regards statistical data, it follows from them that in 2015 these appeals were not much, they were brought in for resolutions concerning the appointment of a judge of not much more than 50, and the Supreme Court repealed seven of these resolutions for re-examination. So we see that here the challenge of these resolutions is small and there is also a small degree of consideration of these appeals by the Supreme Court, so at this stage there is no specially blocking this procedure of appointment of a judge. In the event of a resolution being passed, it shall immediately be submitted to the President, who shall decide whether or not to appoint a certain candidate for a vacant judicial position. As regards the tasks of the National Judicial Council, it is also important to assess the legal acts. In 2015, the National Council assessed a total of over 200 draft laws and regulations, namely 95 draft laws and 117 draft regulations, so it is a significant number of legal acts to which the National Judicial Council referred under its responsibilities and competences. Here I would like to stress that, in principle, we do not benefit from outside assistance, these opinions are rather drawn up by our own forces, using the fact that among the members of the National Council of Courts are judges of all levels, there are judges of administrative courts. So it seems to us that here, to a large extent, we have the opportunity to make a substantive reference to the projects concerning the functioning of the judiciary, which are assessed by the National Judicial Council. The issue of disciplinary liability of judges is also an important issue. This is one of the important issues that the National Judicial Council is dealing with. I would like to remind you that this is in such a way that all decisions which are given by disciplinary courts are presented to the members of the National Judicial Council by the committees on disciplinary liability of judges. They are examined in plenary and the National Judicial Council decides whether those decisions which are unlawful and subject to action would be challenged by the National Judicial Council because the National Judicial Council has the power to file such appeals. With regard to disciplinary judgments, their number has remained approximately constant for several years. For example, in 2014, the disciplinary courts of first instance issued 84 judgments, in 2015, 72 judgments, so here there is no significant difference, but the number of those judgments fell. The 2015 National Judicial Council challenged 12 such judgments, including 10 against and two against the accused judges. As regards the important issue addressed by the National Judicial Council, this is also the decision to move the judge to rest. In 2015, the Council The National Judicial Council issued 31 resolutions on the transition of judges to retirement. In practice, as I said earlier at the committee meeting, this applies to situations where judges have experienced severe, serious diseases. The procedure thus appears to have the opinion of experts drawn up in such proceedings. If it appears from these opinions that the judge is unable to continue to exercise his office, that is the basis for his retirement. Fortunately, there are not many cases, but I would like to stress that unfortunately this is often the case for younger and younger judges, which is probably associated with a lot of stress and work overload, because in some courts the situation is that the judge has as many as 1,000 judges. keep it up to date in his paper. So it is such a burden that some judges have a problem dealing with this burden somehow meaningfully. I would also like to signal that the National Judicial Council is also a member of the founder of the European Judicial Council Network and participates in the work of the ENCJ. This year there was even an annual meeting of these delegates in Warsaw, which was in some way an appreciation of the role and importance of the National Council of Judicial Affairs in this institution. Maybe so much basic statistical information for the state. If there was a need to fill in some data, I am, of course, prepared to present them to you. I didn't want to refer to all of these statistics, because it wouldn't have given us an idea of the basic tasks and what the national council really does. Thank you.
