ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u294	Mr. Marshal! (PL) Mr President, I would like to ask you a few questions. High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform would like to present our position on information on the activities of the National Judicial Council in 2015. I would like to start by quoting: The regime of the Republic of Poland is based on the division and balance of legislative power, executive power and judicial authority. The legislative authority is exercised by the Sejm and the Senate, by the President of the Republic of Poland and by the Council of Ministers, and by the courts and tribunals - art. 10 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The balance between the different types of government, underlined in its content, enables a democratic state to function properly. Moreover, the same basic law in the article. 173 says that courts and tribunals are separate and independent from other authorities. The guardian of the independence of the courts and independence of judges in the Polish legal system is the National Council of the Court of Justice. The Constitutional Tribunal is also, as stressed in its judgment of 12 December 2012, a constitutionally empowered representation of the judicial authority. It can be said that it is obvious, but in recent times, when these threats to the independence of courts and independence of judges are increasing, and the division, balance and independence of power are increasingly disturbed, this becomes particularly important. All the more so as we hear more and more very disturbing, emerging information that the Polish justice system was waiting under the pretext of reorganisation verification of judges. One of the most important tasks, if not the most important task placed before the National Judicial Council in the Constitution, is to examine and evaluate candidates for the judicial office and to present applications for the appointment of judges to the President of the Republic of Poland. It is the KRS that points to people worthy of this office, both in terms of professionalism, knowledge and integrity, i.e. the guarantee of maintaining the principle of independence. The selection procedure for the judge's office is based on a competition and the procedure is multi-stage. So first, the candidacy is evaluated by a team consisting of both judges and parliamentarians and a representative of the President of the Republic of Poland, appointed from among the members of the Council, and the final election is made by the whole board in plenary. It is also worth noting that the resolutions of the Board taken in individual cases, whether positive or negative, must be duly justified, whereas the applicant has the right to challenge them. In addition, where several persons apply for a post, such justification must include information on what criteria have been taken into account when comparing candidates. The point is that there is no doubt that the Council has considered all the circumstances relevant to the resolution of the case comprehensively. Therefore, while remaining on the subject of the examination and evaluation of candidates for the performance of the judicial office, it is impossible not to refer to the current situation, i.e. the refusal of the President of the Republic of Andrzej Duda to appoint 10 judges elected by the National Judicial Council. As I have already mentioned, candidates for judicial offices, and in particular their knowledge, skills and predispositions to pursue this profession, are carefully checked in the framework of the competition procedure. It is attended by a representative of the President of the Republic of Poland. The refusal of appointment, and without any substantive justification, raises concerns that the decision is politically based and bears the mark of an infringement of the independence of the courts. It seems that those who wish to become judges or who are seeking a promotion to a higher court must not only pass a transparent competition procedure, which is obvious, but also an assessment on the basis of which criteria are made by the President's officials, and it is the President, in fact a politician, who makes the final decision to appoint. Such conduct can also be seen as an attempt to pressure that, in the event of judgments being taken, such a person has no chance of engaging in activities that are not necessarily governing the career of a judge. It has already been mentioned - among the most important competences of the National Council of Courts is also the opinion of normative acts concerning the judiciary and judges and the request to the Constitutional Court to examine the compatibility of normative acts with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland as regards the independence of courts and independence of judges. This is not just about the final legislation already adopted, but about the projects that have been created throughout the legislative process. This right entails the obligation for the Council to propose amendments to legal acts relating to the judiciary so that it can comment on them. Statistics have already been cited here. As I said before, in the catalogue of competences belonging to the National Judicial Council is among others. the right to apply to the Constitutional Tribunal to examine the constitutional compatibility of normative acts in so far as they concern the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. Last year, the National Judicial Council exercised this power, among others. In the context of the Act of 19 November 2015 amending the Constitutional Tribunal Act, as well as the Act of 22 December 2015 amending the Constitutional Tribunal Act, indicating that it is wholly incompatible with a number of articles of the Constitution. The arguments cited there were inter alia: that the legal arrangements put in place prevent the Constitutional Court from acting in a manner independent of the legislative and executive authority and constitute an obstacle for the Constitutional Tribunal to carry out effective and reliable control of normative acts as regards their compatibility with the Constitution, leading to the... The Constitutional Tribunal. I can't agree with the views that the National Judicial Council is somehow blaming. It is difficult to have any comments on the National Judicial Council for exercising its constitutional powers. If there are any doubts on the compatibility with the constitution of a given legal act, who is more entitled to resolve these doubts than the Constitutional Tribunal? I have the impression that these statements by the representatives of the Law and Justice are trying to involve the National Judicial Council in a political dispute and they are not going to discuss with the judicial community about important problems affecting it, and this is expressed in the absence of an invitation, the non-participation of any representative of the President or the President himself, the Prime Minister or representatives of the government in the congress held on Saturday, and the saying at present that there were opposition representatives, and the banishing of this invitation, while the invitation itself was not accepted. In conclusion, on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I would like to address the requests raised in this report. A proposal to organise an appropriate discussion forum prior to the drafting of specific legislative proposals is reasonable, so that the quality of the legislation is as high as possible. It seems worth introducing a solution to allow the President of the court to apply to the court of superiors to refer certain cases to another less burdensome court, and to include judges at rest, of course, if their health permits. An important issue is certainly the issue of initial training preparing for the profession of judge and continuing training for judges and referenda. Those demands that concern the staffing needs of courts and the provision of modern technologies to improve the work of courts should be considered very seriously. Finally, I would like to address the following point. Although the President of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights did not comment on the law amending the Act on the National Judicial Council and certain other laws which we will probably be discussing shortly, I do not think that the proposal to put out the term of office of the existing members of the National Judicial Council before the expiry of their four-year term, which, in my view, is in line with the principle of continuity of constitutional action by the state authorities, or the proposal to change the function assigned to the President in the Constitution to appoint the President to serve as a free judge in the event of the application of more than one candidate, consisting in the presentation by the Council to the President for the election of at least two candidates, is not a good solution and they agree to the foundations of a democratic state. Mr. Marshal! (PL) Mr President, I would like to ask you a few questions. High Izbo! I would like to say that according to the Civic Platform Club, the National Judicial Council has properly implemented its constitutional powers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

[[Voice from the room: bound.]] The main role and primary task of the National Judicial Council is to uphold the independence of the courts and independence of the judges and this has been expressed directly in the content of the Article. 186 constitutions. The National Judicial Council appeared in our public life in 1989 after a round table. It was created as a guardian and guarantor of the independence of the courts and independence of judges and found its constitutional basis already in 1989, which the 1997 Constitution significantly extended. Legal meaning of the formula used in Article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the Constitution consists, first, of emphasising the axiological rank of both principles mentioned therein. Using the term "guard stand', actually reserved in the Constitution for the protection of values of fundamental importance to the existence of the State, not only was the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges given special importance, but also the obligation to protect their environment. Secondly, the term "guard" cannot be understood only in its defensive aspect, i.e. as an integral part of the Board's task only to counter the violations of the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. In practice, it is important to ensure that the Council participates in decisions concerning courts and judges in order to remove the possibility of such infringements. This is particularly about personal decisions concerning judges. Today's understanding of the principles of independence and independence precludes leaving the executive authority free to decide whether to appoint, promote or transfer judges. That is why the rank of this body has been elevated to constitutional level. The concept of a threefold division of power into a judicial, executive and legislative body is intended to ensure the mutual balance of the authorities and prevent the exercise of judicial functions by the executive and legislative authorities. The division of power and the principle of mutual balancing and braking prevent abuse of power. The principle of three divisions of power does not eliminate the links between the judicial authority and the executive or legislative authority, although the separation and independence of the judicial authority must be preserved. The existing links must not interfere in any way with its essence, must not deprive the courts of independence and the judges of the quality of independence. Such separation of judicial authority results from the assignment to the courts of a specific task of exercising justice. The selection procedure for judges is strictly defined and has statutoryly clear criteria for the Council to select the best candidate in this selection process. And this is one of the main tasks and main tasks of the National Judicial Council - presenting applications to the President for appointment to serve as a judge. However, the election of these persons shall be made by the Council, presenting the candidates for their appointment to the President. It is up to the National Judicial Council to be responsible and responsible for the choice of persons as judges. Such a person may not be chosen by another authority, in particular it may not be an executive authority, because there is no such authority. The President does not have the authority to choose the persons to become members of the judicial community, but merely appoints the judge of the persons elected by the National Judicial Council. This concerns both the guarantees of professionalism and the possession of the so-called. "incorruptible character" and such qualities that allow, inter alia, to be accepted, that as judges, they will be guided by the principle of independence. In this context, I would like to refer to the order of the President of the Republic of Poland of June this year. the refusal to appoint 10 judges designated by the Council. The spokesperson of the KRS, Judge Waldemar Żurek, commented on the case, expressed the view that it was the KRS who chose it, and the President merely appointed the judges. Such an approach, which cannot be agreed with, would lead the president to the role of a certain notary, the master of the ceremony, which celebrates the choice of judges. In the meantime, the appointment of judges should be regarded as a prerogative of the President of the Republic. He has every right to refuse his appointment. The question is whether a refusal should involve a statement of reasons. This would certainly avoid suspicion of arbitraryness. The fact is, however, that the essence of the three divisions of the authorities is the control system between the different authorities in accordance with the check and balance principle. The judicial authority is not excluded from this mechanism and cannot become a specific state in the State. The Council's approach to the presidential prerogative of appointing or refusing to appoint judges is all the more surprising when we recall another piece of information. We read that: The implementation of the most important KRS competence was hindered by the fact that most people aspiring to the judge's office did not have an independent legal practice. Therefore, there was a lack of a very important element to assess their suitability for judicial functions, which is the ability to apply law in practice. Furthermore, the Council was not able to verify the personality and character of candidates for the judge's office, to verify their decision-making or to treat people with regard to the parties to the proceedings, the judges' composition or the employees of the court, as this possibility can only be given by the court and the courtroom. In addition, the Council refers to problems in the operation of the IT system serving applications for free judicial positions. In the light of these observations, the active role of the President of the Republic in assessing requests for appointment of judges is therefore particularly justified. However, it should be borne in mind that the role of the National Courts should not be reduced to assess the suitability of candidates to serve as judges. Taking into account the article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the Constitution, it should be necessary to create the conditions for the Council to co-form the process of preparing candidates during the judicial application at the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, as well as to supervise the lifelong learning process after the nomination. It is also necessary to prepare an amendment to the Law on the System of General Courts, especially as regards precise rules concerning the supervision of the Minister of Justice over the administrative activities of the courts. In conclusion, the Council's requests for advice on current problems and the needs of the justice system, mentioned on pages 57.59, should in most cases be considered justified. In particular, it should be considered necessary to ensure that a lasting system solution is put in place, which will allow for an increase in the level of remuneration of auxiliary staff in the general courts in the coming years, in particular the assistants of judges. Unfortunately, the report lacks information on cases of breaches of the principles of professional ethics by judges in the context of their involvement in current political activities and on the actions taken by the National Council of the Court of Justice as a result of its statutory obligation to ensure compliance with those principles, as well as the reasons for the Council's failure to react to the non-constitutional actions of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, which makes the report part-time. In addition, it should be pointed out that the involvement of the board in organising the Congress of Judges is a commitment to the current political dispute, which should not be the case. We have some proposals here that are able to improve this, but I would like to say that there is a need for determination and a reasonable course of action for years, because this cannot be done within a year, but we are dealing with such a situation that at a time when we already have free Poland, a democratic system, there were more than 20 ministers of justice at that time, which is practically, statistically the Minister of Justice does not occupy its office for more than a year, we have such a problem that e.g. The Constitutional Act on the National Council of Courts has been amended almost 100 times and a reform is being proposed every now and then to improve the functioning of courts, such as: bringing small courts together in large, winding down certain courts. We know what a fuss, for example. caused the so-called. Minister Gowin's reform, which consisted in the liquidation of the smallest courts, was to be a wonderful remedy for the functioning of the judiciary. So, as a judge, we know that the judiciary needs reform, only that the reform of sense and done with judges, and that is what we propose, we have concrete proposals. We are aware that one of the fundamental problems that negatively affects the assessment of justice is the chronicity of proceedings, but of course I am aware that in many cases it is possible to say that a particular judge is guilty, that a particular court is guilty and that proceedings are taking too long, only that, ladies and gentlemen, we are currently dealing with such a situation that the number of cases has increased avalanchely in the courts and that the number of judges has been around the same level for 10 years. Last year, the impact of the cases on the courts increased by 400,000. and it has exceeded 15 million, so the question arises as to whether, in the case of such a number of cases affecting the courts, the proceedings will be conducted smoothly. One of the fundamental demands that the judge's environment claims is to assess the cognition of the courts, because it raises a fundamental question whether all these cases that go to the courts really have to go to the courts and be recognized in such a way that, for example. the court shall rule in first and second instance with full harassment regardless of the nature of the proceedings. The postulate on the limitation of cognition was, practically speaking, at the head of the postulates that were reported at the congress, and was included in the congress resolution. The second fundamental issue is the simplification of procedures. Our procedures are generally too complicated, even for professionals, qualified lawyers, lawyers or lawyers, they are difficult to control, let alone if we were to bring an average citizen to justice and he was to deal with such a procedure. However, there is the possibility of simplifying procedures, removing parts of separate proceedings, because there are too many of them, so there are relatively simple solutions and examples are not difficult. I'd like to mention, for example. the situation quite obvious, relating to the cassation procedure, when we are dealing with a very strange legislative solution, which is that the person who benefits from the exemption from judicial costs in the ordinary court does not benefit from this exemption at the stage of filing the cassation complaint and benefits from the exemption at a later stage in enforcement proceedings. This is a very strange solution. It is enough to amend one procedural provision so that at least this stage of the cassation procedure significantly simplifies and accelerates, because it forces the citizen to apply again for exemption from legal costs when submitting a cassation complaint. This is one such example, where in a clear way, it seems to me, we see that even a simple simplification can result in a very large change. I would like to stress that the judicial community responded to the appeal of the Ministry of Justice made at the end of last year, because the Ministry asked us this question as to whether we wanted to participate in the reform of the justice system and we see some sensible solutions. Such solutions have been notified, but so far the Ministry of Justice has not disclosed whether or not it takes them into account and whether it intends to take them into account. So I would like to declare here that the problem of poor social evaluation of the functioning of justice is a very important and priority issue for us. We want to do a lot to make this public perception better. And that slogan that was given at the congress, which is the right to court, wasn't accidentally found there, because we meant as judges to signal that the courts were for the citizen. This is how the justice system needs to be reformed so that every citizen has the right to have access to court and to a fair trial, so that all judges care. I also wanted to correct a statement here that the National Judicial Council was the organizer of this congress. The National Council was a co-organizer of this congress. He was organized jointly by the National Judicial Council and the judicial associations, or actually at the request of the judicial associations, which bring together almost half of the judges and know what the expectations of the environment are. The judges specifically asked that such a convention be held so that they could declare their willingness to reform the justice system, but also to protect the independence, independence of judges, which are the basis of the democratic system of our state. With regard to the issue of social control or, in general, the assessment of the justice system, it seems to me that here the position and the National Judicial Council, and the judiciary itself, are clear. We clearly claim that the activities of the courts and all judicial decisions are subject to criticism. All we're asking is for this to be factual criticism. If criticism is that someone says that the judgment of the court is defective, because it is, for example. It is not in accordance with the letter of law, or it is not in accordance with the generally accepted interpretation of the law, that we never treat it as an attack on the justice system. We consider such criticism to be fully permitted and necessary. On the other hand, if criticism is based on the fact that the critic is limited to stating who the mother was or who the father of the judge who gave the ruling was, then we believe that this has nothing to do with criticism, and we are protesting against such an assessment of the functioning of the judiciary. I would also like to clarify that, as the National Council of Courts, we are similarly assessing the functioning of the legislative and executive authority, because we assume that in a democratic legal state all authorities are subject to control, no one is above the law. So if the National Judicial Council evaluates some legislation that comes from parliament, it does not do so for political purposes. I would ask you not to assign such intentions to the National Judicial Council. We only speak about the law and use only substantive arguments. If, among those positions presented by the National Judicial Council, there is a statement which does not refer to legal arguments, then of course we will agree with all those who criticised such a position. I hope you can't tell this kind of situation. We are really trying, ladies and gentlemen, to make a purely substantive assessment. The fact that we are criticizing the act of the current parliamentary majority is not an attack on executive power. If we are criticizing a government project, it is not a criticism of the current government. In these documents, which the National Judicial Council prepares, we never refer to the name of the governing party, to the determination of the majority from this point of view. So I understand that some may be critical of the solutions proposed by the current parliamentary majority as entanglement in a political dispute, but there is really no intention on the part of the National Judicial Council, we want to speak about the law and use legal arguments. If, in response to this, Parliament's representative says that we are wrong and makes a legal argument, it seems to me that this is the right level of debate, and then there will be no charge that we are politically involved on any side. Once again, we don't have that kind of intention. As far as the problem of actions threatening the independence of courts and attacks on the judiciary is concerned, I have already tried in part, practically speaking, to answer that question. If such statements arise which are not of a substantive nature, they relate to personal issues, they are so dangerous that they can cause so-called. the freezing effect, because it may turn out that when examining the next case someone will be afraid of this kind of criticism and will not fully rule. So there is a danger in that. Such action is not in line with standards. It is stressed that, of course, in a democratic state, the criticism of the judiciary is acceptable, but it should be of a substantive nature, and in such a case, when criticism comes from representatives of other authorities, it should be moderate, it should take into account that criticism may later raise doubts about the independence of the courts. We never questioned the very possibility of criticism. With regard to the problem of supervision of the functioning of the judiciary, the administrative supervision currently exercised by the Minister of Justice, this is indeed a major problem of the public interest. The National Judicial Council considers that the current model of administrative supervision of courts is not a good solution, especially in a situation where the functions of Minister of Justice and the Prosecutor General have been merged. This involves not only the merger of these offices. As we remember, the attorney general's procedural powers have been increased at the same time. Potentially, the Attorney General may be a party to court proceedings that are pending may order specific procedural proceedings by the Prosecutor. We don't think it's a good idea for a prosecutor who is potentially a party to legal proceedings to exercise administrative oversight of the courts at the same time. There are experiences from other countries to which we would like to appeal, experience of the administrative judiciary, which is not subject to the supervision of the Minister of Justice and therefore does not function any worse than common justice. We believe that such a solution, in which administrative supervision of courts would be entrusted, for example. the first President of the Supreme Court, with a view to guaranteeing the independence of the judiciary, would be a better solution than the present one. The question also concerned the congress and the absence of representatives of power. I'd like to say that I'm not taking on the role of congressional spokesperson. I could only refer to this from the point of view of the National Judicial Council as a co-organizer of the Congress and explain that the organisers wanted to invite the representatives of the authorities, because we thought it was a good place for the representatives of the legislative and executive authorities to familiarize themselves with the positions of judges. We thought this would be a proper forum for debate. The previous congress, as I said earlier on the committee, took place under the supervision of the president. It was attended by the Minister of Justice, by the Prime Minister, by the Marshal of the Sejm. The invitations were addressed to all of these people. So the fact that they only partially benefited the heads of parliamentary clubs, of course, upset the judges, because we think it would be better if such a declaration of cooperation with the executive and legislative authorities at this congress could be reported directly. Here I would also like to signal that the division and balance of the authorities, which are the basis of our system, assumes not only the separation of these authorities, but their cooperation. So we clearly want the relationship between the judicial authority and the other authorities to take into account the element of cooperation established by the legislator. That is why, for example, we have spoken to the President - thank you for inviting the President to talk to the President's bureau - precisely about the appointments of judges, because we believe that there is a possibility here to talk about this, that there is a way of working in such a situation, when the President, I apologize, Mr President, has reservations about some candidacy. In advance of the question that has been raised here, the National Judicial Council has not yet taken an assessment position, as we should finally understand the President's prerogatives regarding the appointment of judges. This will be the subject of the meeting this month and the national council will take the stand. This position, which was referred to by Judge Żurek, was a position from 2007 - here one of the Members mentioned it - so it is not the current position of the Council. The question was also whether the National Judicial Council is taking any action to eliminate unfair judges by profession, or whether it takes this seriously. I would like to say that the issues of the ethical behaviour of judges are of particular interest to the Council. There is a permanent committee on the ethics board. The National Judicial Council has adopted a code of ethics. In such a symbolic way, we even hand over this code to every newly appointed judge in order to emphasize that this is a matter which is very important to the National Courts Council. Any case in which we have any information on the wrong behaviour of the judge or the allegations in this regard shall be dealt with by the Ethics Committee and the National Judicial Council in such situations where the circumstances of the judge's behaviour raise doubts, shall also make proposals to the disciplinary hearing officer for the initiation of an investigation. And here, too, one of the Members said that in these disciplinary proceedings, the charges mainly concern speeding. It's true that such disciplinary decisions happen. The second group of these disciplinary proceedings, where there are a lot of judgments declaring the fault of the judges, is the timeless drafting of the statements, but I have to say with regret that there are also disciplinary proceedings where these offences are more serious and disciplinary decisions are taken where the judges are transferred to other courts or removed from their duties as a disciplinary judge. Thus, any such case where an unworthy judge who does not allow him to remain in office ends in expulsion from the service and such cases of disciplinary decisions have been a dozen in recent years. One of the Members' questions concerned the length of proceedings. As I said at the outset, we are aware that this is one of the fundamental things that negatively affects the image of the judiciary. As the National Judicial Council - the judicial community also has such demands - we are proposing changes that would reduce the duration of these proceedings. I would like to point out that this is also largely due to the uneven burden on courts of the influence of cases, because we are usually talking about statistics which indicate that, for example. there are 15 million cases affecting courts, but in fact in larger cities, in larger courts these cases have a statistically significant impact, of course, much more than the average, which is still very large. In view of this, one of the main problems is how to solve this problem, so that this large impact of cases is at least evenly balanced, that is to say, to evenly burden the courts with the influence of cases. This is what we are considering and we would like to make some proposals in this regard, because we are aware that a mechanism such a more balanced burden on the impact of cases should be sought. This is one of the forms, and the other is to limit the cognition of cases affecting courts, because we think that it's really, practically speaking, difficult to talk about some real shortening of the length of court proceedings when in larger courts judges have 1,000 or even more cases in their paper. If someone has 1,000 cases lying on the shelf waiting to be resolved, then even with the addition, I don't know how much diligence there is practically no way to identify them within a reasonable period of time that would be satisfactory to the parties. We believe that this needs to be considered seriously, the procedures that provide that, in fact, every case and every dispute, whatever the subject matter of this procedure, must be dealt with by the courts in two instances with certain procedural guarantees. I'm sure that's something to think about. It seems that a large part of the cases would not have to be dealt with by the courts in this way. With regard to the problem of long-term referrals in probably inter-institutional proceedings, I would like to stress that this is probably also related to various procedural issues which should be considered. I've already talked about this case, for example. applying for exemption from judicial costs, which extends the time of referral. This is the problem of the deadline for making excuses. In a situation of heavy burden, a large number of cases not all judges write justifications in time, so the problem of reducing the burden in courts would have an impact in many respects on the possibility of shortening these proceedings. The problem of misunderstood independence of judges. The Member said that the judges did not want to react to the comments, some significant reservations about the procedure, hiding themselves from their independence. Indeed, it can be assumed that there are cases where the notion of independence or independence is misunderstood, because it should generally be a matter of judgment, whereas as regards organisational issues concerning the functioning of courts, it is not every case in which, for example, it is contested. the way in which cases are dealt with, the way in which cases are dealt with, and the time in which cases are dealt with, can be qualified in such a way that this is a simple reconciliation in the independence of the judge. He also asked about this situation when there was a positive opinion about a prosecutor who was a candidate for a judge's position and the National Judicial Council did not take this candidacy into account. So, generally speaking, the fact that the assembly of judges misjudged this candidacy is an element that is taken into account, but it is not a decisive element. However, I would like to add that I do not know the system in any European country, where in such cases, for example. the limitation institution shall be excluded. This is the penalty for the specific acts that are being committed in this area. I am about to mention some of the details that you have touched on, but I will also remind you that, as the Ministry, we have supported the parliamentary project. It was a draft law on amending the Penal Code Act and it was about extending the limitation periods of criminal offences by either 5 years or 10 years. In the case of 5 years, it was a matter of actions that were punishable by a sentence not exceeding 3 years imprisonment, and in the case of 10 years it was another offence. This amendment entered into force in March under the law which was passed by the Sejm in January, just the law on amending the Law of the Penal Code. At the moment, however, we are preparing changes - they are at the stage of internal consultation - which concern e.g. VAT fraud, economic crime. It's about a project that, among other things, is. provides for a specific form of qualified crime, crimes against property, against economic turnover. Here, indeed, in the case of multiples of the amount that determines the value of the property, we provide for very severe penalties, depending on what the act is: whether against property or against economic turnover. It may be a punishment of at least 3 years, but we also provide for a punishment of 25 years imprisonment. We predict in the project, among others. the introduction of Article. 306a Penal Code, which e.g. provides that, in the case of individual offences against economic turnover, property of more than 10 times the amount of the value of the offence shall be punishable by imprisonment for a period of not less than 3 years. So I would like to point out that we are not undermining this problem precisely because of what the Supreme Audit Chamber has claimed to notice. In one of her reports she pointed out that there are very large amounts that we just lose because of crime, these are tens of billions of zlotys. He is robbed, I am robbed, every one of us is robbed, citizens are robbed, from which came a project which actually provides for far-reaching penalties. As regards the institution of limitation in this case, taking into account the arrangements currently applied in Article. 101 and Article 101 102 of the Penal Code, in the event of a major economic scandal, we will have a statute of limitations which will take place after 30 years. However, it does not seem a good idea to eliminate at all, so that the limitation does not apply in such cases at all, because, as I said at the beginning, this is a completely different area of crime. I personally do not know the European system, any country where this institution would be excluded. But we're definitely tightening our punishments. Thank you very much. [[Thank you very much.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u375	Minister! Maybe I'll start with your marshal. I'm so sorry, Minister. Mrs. Marshal! On holiday abroad, we take with us the European Health Insurance Card. Thanks to the emergency insurance card, Poles can benefit from medical assistance under universal health insurance in the European Union and EFTA countries. The European Health Insurance Card shall be issued free of charge and in the event of a positive confirmation of the rights at hand. A person with such insurance shall have the right to medical benefits necessary during his or her temporary stay in the country concerned. The extent of the benefits shall be decided by the doctor of the country concerned. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u376	Thank you very much. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Mr Piotr Gryza. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u377	Dear Marshal! (PL) Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! To answer this question, I would first like to inform you that the rules on the clearance of residence and health insurance of persons insured in another country, and in EU countries, and in EFTA countries, are laid down in the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the coordination of the social security system. This principle generally boils down to such an important rule that we receive those benefits that they benefit from in the country concerned. Since the schemes are different, we benefit from those benefits that citizens benefit from, so health benefits provided under the European Health Insurance Card are carried out on the same terms and to the same extent as those applied to insured persons in the country concerned. This means that a person who has received an EKUZ card can benefit in the country of residence only from health care benefits covered by a basket of guaranteed benefits as defined in the country in question, i.e. in Switzerland dental treatment is not refunded under general health insurance and we cannot receive this benefit. This also means that the holder of the EKUZ card, which goes beyond the borders of Poland, is obliged to pay additional fees from his own resources if this is provided for under the insurance system. In many Member States of the European Union and the EFTA States, the own contribution of insured persons to the costs of providing health services shall apply. In a large part of these countries there is a partial payment of various amounts, and in a large part there is an outpatient treatment. In several countries, i.e. in France, Belgium, Luxembourg, the principle is to bear by patients the full costs of outpatient treatment, which are then refunded by sick money, but not in full amount e.g. in France, about 70%. The Polish insured person may also apply for such a refund directly to the cashier of the sick on the spot or after returning to the country to the National Health Fund, with the National Health Fund refunding only the part of the costs borne by the health insurance institution in the country of residence. Urgent hospital treatment on the basis of the EHIC card is exempted from fees in the following countries: Cyprus, Denmark, Spain, the Netherlands, Ireland, Iceland, Lithuania, Malta, Norway, Romania, Slovakia, Hungary, Italy. In other EU and EFTA countries, hospital treatment is subject to very different charges. The highest fees apply in Switzerland, France, Finland, Luxembourg, Belgium and Slovenia, for example. in Germany we pay for the day of your stay, so if your stay in the hospital lasts for a while, then we have to pay the rate times the number of days we were in the hospital. Detailed information is available on the National Health Fund website. If we go to the main page, there's a bookmark: Treatment outside the country. In the International Health Fund Cooperation Department tab, which acts as a liaison body, there is a sub-book on treatment during the holidays, in which these issues are specified, an interactive map and details of the characteristics of the insurance system as regards access to benefits and possibly additional costs in each of these countries are provided. The question was also about the problems Poles are meeting, who are leaving with the EKUZ card. This is a review that was done for the purpose of this question, because the National Health Fund regularly presents statistics to the Administrative Commission for Health. Coordination of the Social Security System with the European Commission. The greatest number of problems due to the scale of our citizens' contacts are encountered in Germany. Here are some examples. German doctors are used to the fact that the German health insurance card has a chip, and our EKUZ card doesn't. There are also language problems. Although there is one card pattern, sometimes the doctor who provides the service does not know about it and there are some problems with providing the service. In any such case, if it turns out that our citizen has encountered irregularities in the health security system in the country in which he was staying, he should report to the National Health Fund. The National Health Fund as a liaison body shall be entitled to enforce the rights deriving from the EHIC Charter. This is not only a matter of financial clearance, but also a matter of both state-level discussions - and state-level discussions - at the level of this administrative committee. I hope it's my answer. [[♪ The bell ♪]] will be satisfying and exhausting. Thank you very much.

The question is asked by the Members: Mrs Anna Milczanowska and Mr Michał Dworczyk, the Law and Justice Club, on legislative action aimed at pointing out whether a civil company can be a real estate tax payer and taking other steps to harmonise judicial case law - to the Ministry of Finance. The first question is asked by Anna Milczanowska. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u403	Yes, it will be, Your Marshal, one question. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Under the current legislation, real estate tax taxpayers are natural persons, legal persons, organisational entities, including companies without legal personality. However, interpretation doubts arise as to whether a civil company is an organisational unit capable of being a real estate tax taxpayer. This problem has been the subject of many judgments. As a result, there is a clear distinction between the legal lines of the administrative courts. The courts of southern Poland tend to claim that a civil company is a taxpayer of real estate tax, while the courts of northern Poland present a different position. The Supreme Administrative Court also lacks a firm settlement on this issue. The different case law in this case adversely affects the sense of the rule of law of economic operators in the form of a civil partnership. The fact that a tax authority determines whether a civil company is a taxable person in the case of a particular tax is dependent on a number of tax consequences. The different legal regime therefore has a significant impact on the way entrepreneurs operate in the form of a civil company. The lack of certainty in the application of the law in this area justifies concrete legislative steps to address the identified irregularities and hence this question, which the Marshal wanted to read at the outset. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u404	Thank you very much, Member. The question is answered by the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Finance, Mr Wiesław Janczyk. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u405	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, According to Art. 3 (1) (a) 1 The Act on Local Taxes and Charges is based on natural persons, legal persons, organisational units, including companies without legal personality. The fulfilment of the condition as to the organisational form does not prejudice the status of a real estate tax taxable person, since it is also necessary to demonstrate the legal title of ownership of the property or building mentioned in that provision. Such titles are: ownership, ownership, perpetual use, possession of property of a local government unit or State Treasury. In the context of the above, a civil company is not a real estate tax taxable person as it cannot acquire rights, including property ownership, on its own behalf, as it cannot become a self-owner of real estate, a permanent owner of land or a owner of property of a local government unit or State Treasury. For the purposes of the Civil Code, a civil company is a contractual obligation in which the partners undertake to provide a common economic objective in accordance with Article. 860 Civil Code. A civil company is therefore a contract rather than a separate legal entity. The Supreme Court identified the civil company as a multilateral obligation relationship between the shareholders. The lack of legal personality of a civil company under the Act on Taxation and Local Charges is therefore due to its essence in the civil process. Consequently, the status of a taxable person is deprived of civil companies, including in the field of personal income tax. The civilistic concept of a civil company whose rights and obligations are governed by its partners justifies that they are taxable persons for property tax, due on immovable property or construction works brought into that company. It should be pointed out that, according to the views of the judicature, the property transferred to a civil partnership becomes the common property of the shareholders. This is a specific form of joint ownership of all partners. The ownership of the property is therefore subject to joint ownership by the members of the civil partnership. Co-owners in joint ownership, e.g. the plot, land, may be only partners of a civil partnership - persons with legal personality, not the contract of those persons concerning the joint economic activity, the contract of a civil partnership. On the other hand, when a partner makes a contribution to the company in the form of the use of immovable property, without at the same time giving up its ownership rights, the property tax obligation is on that partner alone, since the property does not enter the company's assets. This view of the non-compliance of a civil company with real estate tax and of the subject-matter of the real estate tax on the shareholders of these companies is supported by a large part of the latest administrative court rulings issued in 2013.2016. That's why we think this division is maybe less geographical and more chronological, in connection with the analysis of these judgments, as you also mentioned. The position of the Ministry of Finance in the present case was similar and has not changed. However, in some judgments, mainly issued before 2013, a different view is presented and a civil company is considered to be a real estate tax taxpayer. In view of the above, the Minister of Finance, within the framework of the powers conferred by the Tax Order, seeks to ensure uniform application of tax law by tax authorities and tax authorities, intends to issue a general interpretation in the near future concerning the application of the provisions of the Law on local taxes and charges in the context of the subject of property tax on members of civil companies. However, under the authority of the Minister, it is not possible to influence the case law of the courts and to harmonise it. So that I think that this question will also contribute in a positive way to making interpretation more unambiguous and more reliable for all concerned. I think the general interpretation will be published in a very short time. We'll try to do it as soon as possible. Thank you for that question.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u471	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In my statement, I would like to raise a very important problem concerning the employment of temporary workers throughout the country. The rules governing the employment of temporary workers by the employer who is a temporary employment agency are governed by the Act of 9 July 2003 on the employment of temporary workers. In the law, in the article. 20 point 1 shall be entered in the following entry: "In a period of 36 consecutive months, the temporary employment agency employing a temporary worker may direct that worker to perform temporary work for one user employer for a period not exceeding 18 months in total.' In practice, this provision remains only on paper, since an employee who has worked for 18 months is employed by another employment agency, although he continues to provide services in the same workplace. On the example of the Gliwice sub-zone of the Katowice Special Economic Zone, I can say that hundreds, or maybe even thousands of people work for a long time, are employed by labour agencies waiting for employment by a company which they ultimately provide their services. For those employed by the employment agency, this situation leads to a lack of job security, and among women there is even a fear of becoming pregnant, as it can contribute to the loss of employment. In addition, temporary workers do not fully exercise the rights of the Labour Code. The idea, which accompanied the development of the labour agency, was to activate the unemployed, but in time thousands of people all over Poland work on temporary contracts for very long years. In view of this situation, my statement to the High House would like to draw attention to the existing problem and ask for any action to be taken to make such changes in order to put an end to the exploitation of workers mentioned by me. Thank you for your attention.

It was then that in 1980 in the mine of the Manifesto of July - today the movement of Zofiówka - the historical settlement of Jastrzębski was signed. This is the third agreement, after Szczecin and Gdansk, which has been permanently incorporated into the history not only of Jastrzębie-Zdrój, but also of the whole Poland. During the night of 27th to 28th August in the mine the Manifesto of July began a strike of miners solidarityizing with workers from Szczecin and Gdańsk. The miners also added a few of their own to the 21 postulates of the coastal workers. On 29 August, seven more mines joined and was bound, for the first time in Silesia, an inter-company strike committee. Soon in the region 28 mines and other workplaces were struck by a strike like miners from Manifesto in July. On the same day, a government delegation arrived at the miners. The negotiations have begun. September 2 approx. hour 17 further negotiations were undertaken, which ended on 3 September at 3 p.m. 5.40. The finale of these difficult talks was undoubtedly a success. After a dozen hours, a 29 - year agreement was finally signed. This historic compromise gave thousands of workers a chance for better working conditions and was the basis for creating a free democratic state. A large proportion of the demands were made on social issues for workers. Free Saturdays and Sundays, voluntary work on statutory holidays, the establishment of independent trade unions, the inclusion of pylica lungs in occupational diseases - these are just a few of the demands that created new, better working conditions. Today, 36 years after these events of August 1980, we must remember both all protesters and those who fought for freedom and solidarity, both then and throughout the communist repression. When the word "solidarity" was formed in the 1980s, no one thought about what it would mean in 20, 30 and 40 years. Now we can safely say that it is an expression of struggle, pride, unity and community. It's a word that contains a specific content and a story that we can't forget. Today we must ensure that it continues to generate positive energy, dreams and, above all, the Polish pride to continue to combine. Solidarity is the heritage of all Poles. There are no more or less privileged in this regard. Some people forget that today. With this special history in mind, the authorities of the town of Jastrzębie-Zdrój took the idea of creating a Solidarity House, a place to commemorate what is important. This event is a milestone of Polish democracy. At the moment, a number of measures are being taken to put this good idea into practice. There was a project to develop a place where all the activities undertaken in themselves will be the dissemination of the latest history and the memory of the events of the 1980s. The Solidarity House is to become a place of integration and agreement between the various social groups, promoting relations and cooperation. Today, returning to these special moments of history for Silesia and the whole country, I would like to ask you to lean on this initiative, join in spreading this idea and helping the city to implement this important investment for Jastrzebie-Zdroj. I particularly refer these words to the Prime Minister Beata Szydło, who has been in Jastrzębie several times, to the government for financial support for the implementation of this project. At this point I would like to remind you that in 2015 this project was written by the Prime Minister Ewa Kopacz as one of the priorities of the programme for Silesia, Silesia 2.0. I believe that the Solidarity House in Hawk-Zdrój will become a place over-local, socially active not only in our city, in the region, but also in the whole country. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u479	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Do you know what a da Vinci robot is? This is such a state-of-the-art surgical device, using which in neighbouring countries, e.g. in Germany or in the Czech Republic, operations are carried out, in particular urological operations, i.e. for prostate, bladder, kidney or gynaecological cancer. Czechs have nine such robots, so in every hospital where urological operations are performed. They are carried out under the social security system, so the Czech citizens do not have to pay for such an operation. Besides, it's standard there. In Poland we also buy robots. In 2010 the R &amp; D centre in the Wrocław hospital purchased a robot from the Polish Science and Technology Fund in the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, and this year such a robot was purchased by the municipal hospital in Toruń from funds received as a result of the 2015 competition by the National Centre for Research and Development also in the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. Robots were purchased to carry out scientific research. Treatment operations are not done. The National Health Fund does not finance such operations. The Health Technology Assessment and Tarification Agency issued three negative recommendations in 2014, as robotic operations are too expensive and do not increase the availability of treatment and improve the effectiveness of therapy, which I do not agree with. In a hospital in Wroclaw within 5 years, a robot will do Vinci has done a little more than 200 different types of operations as part of the study. And in Czech or German hospitals, they are made about 300 quarterly. The robot doesn't get tired. The teams of surgeons exchange because you're tired. The robot arm won't shake, and the surgeon's arm can. On the screen, the internal organs can be seen in a tenfold magnification. Man's eye isn't that precise. After prostatectomy, the patient is only in hospital for four days. The catheter is removed after 8 days. The investigation into full efficiency is much faster. Urine retention returns within about 1.5 months, up to 2 months, and after a traditional surgery even after more than a year. In more than 98% Robot surgery has no complications, and in the case of young men who have been diagnosed and operated on prostate cancer, sexual functions are also preserved, which is not provided by traditional surgery. I've got a friend who's two years old after a traditional surgery. He's not holding his urine, and he's going to have another surgery. However, the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarifications does not take into account further costs of treatment as a result of many complications, sanatorium treatment, absenteeism at work. However, I hope that this will soon change that robotic surgery will be available as part of patient health services, that they will be included in the evaluation of not only the costs of surgery, but also the costs of all treatment, including treatment of complications, and rehabilitation, because today we are in the field of availability of robotic surgery 100 years back. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-05-sejm-24-1.u481	Statement on the 70th anniversary of Felix Selmanowicz's death. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! August 28th this year The 70th anniversary of Felix Selmanovich's death was over. Zagoń. I would therefore like to say a few words about the life of this enchanted soldier. Felix Selmanowicz was born on June 6, 1904 in Vilnius. His early youth fell during World War I and the struggle for independence. As a volunteer, he and his brother took part in the Polish-Bolshevik war. For the next few years he was associated with Branch II of the General Staff of the Polish Army. In 1923 he moved to a civilian and worked as an office worker. Feliks Selmanowicz was mobilized again on 25 August 1939. He was then sent to the Border Protection Corps. After the aggression of the Soviet Union against Poland, he took part in the fights against the Red Army. When he crossed the border with Lithuania, he was interned by the authorities of the country. After he escaped from the camp, he returned to Vilnius and made contact with the conspiracy. In early 1944 Selmanowicz went to 3. The Vilnius AK Brigade. Gracian Frog of the Szczerby and then moved to 5. The Vilnius AK Major Brigade. Zygmunt the Szendzielarz of Szupęski. He was a deputy platoon commander there. He quickly got promoted to the rank of lieutenant of wartime. In May 1944 he took command of the company in 4. The Vilnius brigade, lieutenant. Longina Wojciechowskiego, Ronina. After disarming, like other soldiers, he was interned in Kaluga. However, he managed to escape from there, return to Vilnius and, thanks to the help of the underground network, return to Poland. Selmanovich made contact with Major at the turn of 1945 and 1946. Then he took command of his own patrol. From March to June, his branch carried out a series of actions, acquiring cash, which was transferred to the branch of Słupzki. On 17 July 1946 Selmanowicz was arrested because of the Żilinski report. He was captured and imprisoned in Sopot. A month later, after a very violent investigation, Zagoń was sentenced to death. On 28 August, he and Danuta were shot in the basement of the Gdansk prison. Before the shots were fired, they both shouted: Long live Poland! The death sentence was annulled in 1997 by a decision of the Provincial Court in Gdansk. Eight years later, IPN reported the likely location of the remains of Felix Selmanowicz. The ceremonial funeral of Zagoń was held on 28 August this year. In the grave next to him was Danuta Sidzikówna.Inka. President Andrzej Duda issued a decree to appoint Felix Selmanovich as Lieutenant Colonel. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
